| 8.27 | Every figure on Investing carries **two decimals**, `.00` included.                                                        | A column where one row reads 33,7 and the next 706,82 does not line up, and the eye reads the shorter number as a different kind of quantity. The exception is an asset priced below a cent, where two decimals would report 0,00 — not a rounder price but a wrong one. Crypto pairs are why that case exists.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| 8.28 | The candle cadence is bounded by a **daily quota**, not by a rate.                                                         | Measured with `scripts/bars-load.py`, straight at the providers: **no refusal at any rate up to 120 requests a minute**, on Yahoo or Binance, with latency flat at ~400ms and ~300ms. Thirty seconds cannot reveal a daily quota, though, which is the shape of limit decision 8.4 is written around — so a clean sweep means "no burst limit found", never "poll as hard as you like". What actually governs upstream load is the **cache TTL**, which absorbs 94–97% of requests; polling faster costs the provider almost nothing and the server almost everything. Lower the TTL before the interval.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 8.29 | A position is a **quantity and an average cost**, not a ledger.                                                            | The migrated data has no history: `inv-portfolio:v1` stores `{qty, costBasis}` per symbol and nothing else. A transaction ledger would start with one synthetic opening lot per position or with years of trades re-entered by hand, and realised P&L is worth having _with_ the data that supports it rather than as an empty frame.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| 8.30 | Positions get their **own panel**, below the watchlist, with a **total per currency**.                                     | The legacy rendered P&L as a badge on the watchlist row, so there was no total and the portfolio was never visible as a whole. Totals are summed per currency because equities quote in USD and pairs in USDT: adding them would be a conversion the code performs silently, at a rate nobody chose. The second line only exists when there is something in it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| 8.31 | A position that has no quote **says so**; it is never valued at zero.                                                      | A total quietly missing one of its holdings is worse than one admitting a row is still loading. Same reasoning as 8.8, where a refused symbol travels beside the ones that worked.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 8.32 | Above 1640px the sidebar is **two columns**; below it, one that scrolls within itself.                                     | Measured, not preferred. Stacked, the watchlist and the positions need **1356px** of height against a 983px viewport, and no amount of shrinking closes that: 20% off every font buys **13%** of height, because most of a row is padding and padding is in `rem`, not `em`. Side by side the taller column is ~721px and both are simply visible, at a cost of 140px of chart canvas — 1183 to 1043. The threshold is where the chart stops being worth having: below roughly 700px of canvas it is a strip.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 8.33 | A long name is **clipped, not shrunk**.                                                                                    | The ellipsis was already there; the grid track was not able to shrink, so `1fr` resolved to the content and the longest name set the column width. `minmax(0, 1fr)` fixed it, and clipping buys 85% of the width a 20% font reduction would buy at no cost in legibility — the symbol above it is the identifier, and the name is a reminder. Worth knowing for any future squeeze: this app's root font is **20px**, not 16, so `0.66rem` is 13.2 CSS pixels, and at the browser zoom in use that is about 8 physical. There is no room below it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 8.34 | The stream's own silence is an **empty batch**, never a cancellation.                                                      | `asyncio.wait_for` around `anext(listener)` delivers its cancellation _into_ the generator, which runs its `finally`, leaves the hub and unsubscribes upstream. So every quiet interval ended the SSE response: the browser reconnected about three seconds later, a still market cost roughly 150 full cycles an hour, and each one re-sent the Yahoo subscribe frame and rebuilt the Binance socket because the followed set momentarily emptied. The wait now lives inside `_Listener.drain`, where what gets cancelled is an `Event.wait` and costs nothing. Verified against the running API: one connection held 70s with three heartbeats, where it previously ended at twenty.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 8.35 | `live` **rises at once and falls slowly**.                                                                                 | It selects the sweep interval, and TanStack rebuilds the refetch timer whenever that changes — so a value flipping faster than the interval it picks means the timer is rebuilt before it ever fires, and the sweep that decision 8.18 makes the source of truth silently stops. 8.34 removes today's cause; the latch removes the class, so no future flap can starve the timer. Falling costs a grace period, rising does not.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 8.36 | An upstream socket is opened only once something is followed.                                                              | Yahoo's connected from the moment the process booted, before any page had asked for a price. Beyond the idle connection, it meant a `TestClient(app)` reached the real network on lifespan — so the endpoint layer could not be tested at all without hitting Yahoo. Binance already waited; Yahoo now does too.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 8.37 | Both paths speak **one session vocabulary**, and `extended` is decided on the server.                                      | Yahoo's REST endpoint says `POSTPOST` and its socket says `POST` for the same session, and the socket also emitted an `EXTENDED` that no consumer had a branch for — so a tick carrying it rendered as though it were a regular-session price. Both map into one set now. `extended` travels on the tick rather than being inferred in the browser, because the REST path already computed it on the server: one question answered in two places is how the vocabularies drifted apart to begin with.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| 8.38 | A **rate limit is reported, never retried wider**.                                                                         | The per-symbol fallback exists for the fragile handshake, and it turns one request into one per symbol. Answering a quota refusal with it multiplies the load tenfold at the exact moment there is none left to spend — against a ceiling decision 8.4 says is daily. A `rate-limited` refusal now travels to the caller as itself, which decision 8.8 already knows how to render beside the symbols that worked. Every other refusal still falls back, since that is what the fallback is for.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 8.39 | **Yahoo survives a datacenter address; Binance does not.** Step 7 must pick its region for crypto.                         | Measured, not assumed — the probe decision 10.6 added, run from a GitHub runner. Every Yahoo surface answered, and **faster than from home**: bars 243ms against 576, the batch 45ms against 422, and the cookie-and-crumb handshake — the fragile one, the thing decision 8.4 was really worried about — 175ms against 758. So the fear was misplaced. What was not predicted at all: **Binance answers 451 to both its REST and its socket**, from an address that Yahoo serves happily. That is geography, not quota, and it means a deploy to that region loses crypto entirely while equities are untouched. The remedy is a region choice or a separate route for pairs, and it is a decision for step 7 to take with this in hand rather than discover afterwards.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 8.40 | Whether an instrument's market closes is **served**, not inferred from a provider's name.                                  | The client answered it with `provider !== 'binance'`, which is reading a provider's name to decide behaviour — the thing decision 8.3 exists to prevent, and the last place it leaked. `hasSession` travels on the bars response now, and `session.ts` no longer knows any provider exists.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 8.41 | A refused row **says why**, in the row.                                                                                    | Decision 8.8 sent the reason all the way to the page and the page dropped it: a rate-limited symbol showed the same `·` as one still loading, forever, so the two were indistinguishable. Rendered in place of the price, muted rather than alarming — it is a fact about that row, not an error the page needs to shout about.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| 8.42 | A provider's refusal carries its **code**, on every endpoint.                                                              | `ProviderError` exists to be machine-readable and says so in its own docstring, but `_as_http_error` sent only the message — so `/quotes` handed the client the word while `/bars` and `/search` left it to infer the reason from an HTTP status. Two endpoints, two answers to one question. Found by the wire tests below, which asserted what the type promised rather than what the code did.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 8.43 | A chart keeps its last window per **symbol and timeframe**, separately from market data.                                   | Pan and zoom are the reader's view, not a property of AAPL or of a candle series. The saved `{first,last}` range therefore sits in its own document, keyed by `symbol:timeframe`: switching AAPL 1d to 1h never borrows an unrelated window. The chart clamps it only after the current bars load, since a provider may roll the returned history forward. Storage hydration completes before the canvas mounts, so it starts at the saved range instead of visibly jumping there. Gesture writes use the shared debounce, which turns a run of pointer frames or wheel events into one durable write.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 8.6  | Alerts compare in-memory quotes to stored rules, and only while the page is open.                                          | Already implied by decision 5.7. Firing with the tab closed needs something running outside the browser, which belongs to the cloud phase rather than to markets.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

### 9. Shared storage

Cross-cutting: Finance and Greenlight both write through `shared/storage`, so these are decided
once rather than twice. Section 6.8 already fixed what the facade guarantees; these say when it
writes and what it says while it is doing so ([#38](https://github.com/edicius2002/edicius-hq/issues/38)).

| ID  | Decision                                                                              | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 9.1 | Edits reach storage on a trailing debounce, and the debounce lives in the facade.     | Measured against the real Finance document: a 30-event drag was 30 writes and 217 KB, and is now one write and 7.2 KB. Putting it in Finance would have left Greenlight writing eagerly and given two answers to one question. The document is written whole, so dragging in one diagram rewrote the other two as well.                                     |
| 9.2 | Local state leads and storage follows.                                                | The node's rendered position used to come back from the write, so the drag only moved as fast as the round trip. At 10 ms locally the queue drains just fast enough to hide it; at 30 ms the input outruns the writes and the node trails the pointer. Step 7 puts this behind a network, so the ordering is the change rather than a consequence of it.    |
| 9.3 | A debounce must be flushable, not only timed: on `pagehide`, on hide, and on unmount. | A timer alone loses whatever it holds when the page goes away, and routing off `/finance` mid-drag would have lost the drag. Flushing on unmount is the case tests would not have caught, because nothing in a test navigates.                                                                                                                              |
| 9.4 | A restore supersedes what the debounce is holding rather than queueing behind it.     | Writing the stale value first only to overwrite it a moment later spends a write to no end, and letting the two race lets the older one land last — a restore silently undone by a drag from before it. Anything already in flight still lands first, because the queue writes one value at a time.                                                         |
| 9.5 | `pending` and `blocked` are states of their own. `syncing` is not implemented.        | A debounce is a window in which your work exists only in memory; saying nothing during it would be the dishonest part of having one. `blocked` has a different remedy from `failed` — reload, not retry — so it is a different word. `syncing` was the legacy's Supabase phase and belongs to step 7; inventing it now would mean a state that never fires. |
| 9.6 | A write that has been overtaken reports nothing.                                      | A generation counter, so a slow response cannot paint "Saved" over edits that are still waiting. It mattered for a round trip before; with a debounce in front it matters for the length of a whole drag.                                                                                                                                                   |
| 9.7 | The test environment refuses an unstubbed `fetch`.                                    | Not a formality. A debounced write can fire after the test that caused it has finished and `vi.unstubAllGlobals()` has put the real `fetch` back, and it then goes to whatever serves the API base URL — in development, the developer's own API over their own data. This happened during the work: a fixture document replaced the real Finance one.      |

### 10. Tooling

| ID   | Decision                                                                         | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ---- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 10.1 | The API passes lint and format in CI, like the web.                              | It only ever ran `pytest`. The gap was visible in the code itself: `# noqa: BLE001` suppressions written for **ruff**, which was never installed — so they suppressed nothing and nobody could tell. Anything not in CI rots quietly; the first run found seven findings in code that had never been checked.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 10.2 | Python tooling runs through the API's own interpreter, never `python` on PATH.   | `python -m ruff` from the repo root picks up whatever happens to be installed, which is how a check passes on one machine and fails on another. `scripts/api.mjs` already resolved the venv for `dev` and `test`; lint and format go the same way.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 10.6 | The datacenter assumption is **probed, not assumed**, and manually.              | Decision 8.4 recorded that requests from the cloud leave a datacenter address rather than a home connection and would be far likelier to be throttled — and then nobody checked. All 4,618 lines of Investing rest on it. `scripts/reachability.py` asks the narrow question that decides whether step 7 is a deploy or a rewrite of the data plane: does each surface answer from here? A GitHub runner is a datacenter address, so the workflow is free; it is `workflow_dispatch` only, because reaching third-party endpoints on every push is the hammering the load script itself warns against. Baseline from a home connection: **all seven surfaces answer**.                                              |
| 10.7 | The root `scripts/` are inside the gates too.                                    | Ruff ran from `services/api`, so the load test and the probe — both Python, both load-bearing for a decision — were outside every check. Anything not in CI rots; that is where 10.1 came from. Twenty-seven files now, not twenty-five.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 10.8 | The **serialization layer** is tested, because that is where the contract lives. | `Quote`, `Bar` and `SymbolHit` are described twice — once in Python, once in TypeScript — and nothing checked the JSON between them. The adapters were well covered and the layer above them was not, so a renamed field would have passed every test and broken the page. `test_wire.py` asserts the literal keys. Its first run found two things: `_as_http_error` dropping the code (8.42), and that the process-global quote cache outlives a test, so a symbol answered in one is served from memory in the next.                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 10.9 | The four places that already knew something failed now **say so**.               | One log line existed in 2,233 lines of API. A Yahoo rate-limit block, a read-only data volume, a stream reconnecting in a loop and a cache at a 0% hit rate all present identically today: prices stop and nothing says anything. Step 7 is the first time this runs where nobody can attach a debugger, and `rate-limited` in particular is decision 8.4's ceiling arriving — invisible from outside the process.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 10.3 | The web lints **with types**, not just with syntax.                              | `tseslint.configs.recommended` without a `projectService` registers the type-aware rules and can never run them — so `no-floating-promises` had never once fired, which is why the codebase is full of hand-written `void` prefixes that nothing was enforcing. Switched to `recommendedTypeChecked`. The first run found 35 problems, of which two were in shipped code: a promise-returning handler in a void-returning prop whose rejection had nowhere to go, and a `String(unknown)` in the watchlist normalizer that would have turned an object into the symbol `[OBJECT OBJECT]` and then polled it. The noisier rules are relaxed for tests only — a fake is allowed to be loose where the product is not. |
| 10.4 | The API is type-checked too, non-strict.                                         | Seven `# type: ignore[...]` comments existed for a checker nobody had installed. mypy's first run said three of them suppressed nothing and two named the wrong error code — the suppressions were decoration. It also found that `_stream` was unannotated, so mypy inferred `None` from the constructor and considered **every line of the upstream integration unreachable**: the whole streaming path was invisible to a checker that was not there. `ignore-without-code` is on, so the next suppression has to name what it is silencing.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 10.5 | What a component needs of a dependency is declared **by the component**.         | The stream adapters took `Callable[..., object]`, which typed nothing and forced the ignores above; the hub took an unannotated `stream`. Both now take a `Protocol` declared where they are consumed, so a test hands over three methods and the type checker can see the rest. `Tick` moved to `adapters/models.py` at the same time: it was the streaming contract living inside the Yahoo adapter, which made the shape of the whole path depend on one provider's file — decision 8.3, leaking.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

### 11. Finance node and panel geometry

A box's height follows what the node says, not what its kind could ever say
([#62](https://github.com/edicius2002/edicius-hq/issues/62)). Measured on the real
24-node document throughout, because every number below changed once it was.

| ID    | Decision                                                                                                           | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 11.1  | Height is a pure function of the node's **content**, and content comes from the document.                          | Every kind was a flat 116px, sized for its worst case, and `align-content: center` turned the difference into air. `selectNodeContent` answers the only three questions height depends on — does this holding carry a fee, how many assets does this account hold, has it seen traffic — from the same data the money maths already reads. So the box follows the text without the layout pass that would make every flow path two-pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 11.2  | Row heights are **derived from the font sizes**, never written down.                                               | The first attempt wrote them down and reserved 15px for a row that takes 15.4 — crushing the row, which is the exact failure the flat sizing existed to avoid. `Math.ceil(font × 1.4)`, and a test asserts each row clears its own line box.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 11.3  | The box is in canvas pixels, so `FlowNode.module.css` states its fonts in pixels.                                  | The stylesheet was in `rem` and the geometry in canvas units; they agreed only at a 16px root font. This app uses 20px, so a holding with a fee needed 120.5px of its 116px box and the grid hid a row. A box measured in canvas units cannot have contents measured in something else.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 11.4  | Asset chips are counted in **lines**, not in assets.                                                               | `.balances` wraps. Reserving a row per asset made the eight-asset account 217px — taller than the flat 116 the change set out to shrink. Two chips per 180px line, the conservative reading of the measured 52–84px widths: over-reserving a line costs 19px, under-reserving hides an asset.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 11.5  | The minimum height is applied **once, after every block is counted**.                                              | Applied inside the row sum instead, a short node paid the floor's padding _and_ its balances on top: a flat 18px of air on every single-line account and job, invisible in the unit tests and obvious the moment it was measured in the browser. Slack across all 24 nodes went from a mean of 8px to 1px, and IBKR from 217px to 134px.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 11.6  | Anything measuring a **set** of nodes takes a `ContentOf` lookup.                                                  | Fit-to-view and the minimap read `contentRect`, and `facingAnchors` picks a side from two centres. With height following the rows, measuring a node as empty bounds a box nobody drew — fit would frame short of the diagram and a connector would leave the wrong edge.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 11.7  | The canvas fills its grid row, and its floor follows the screen.                                                   | It was `clamp(26rem, 58vh, 46rem)` — a band chosen without reference to the column beside it. Below 1400px `.workspace` stacks and there is no row to fill, so the floor is the whole height; a fixed 26rem spent the same 520px on a laptop and a 1440p display. Measured at 1139×802, the page furniture above the canvas takes 529px, so only 273px of the diagram is ever on screen — that, not the canvas height, is the ceiling.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 11.8  | Both side panels hold a **fixed height**, and what does not fit scrolls inside.                                    | Measured across selections: 402px with nothing selected against 482, 541 and 482 for a holding, an account and a job — so every click moved the totals below by up to 139px, and in the two-column layout resized the canvas beside them. A panel that changes size when you look at something moves the thing you were about to click. 28rem and 18rem, taken from those measurements with headroom over the tallest case.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 11.9  | The out fee and the in fee sit **side by side**.                                                                   | They are the two halves of one question — what a transfer costs leaving and what it costs arriving. Stacked, they read as two unrelated settings that happen to be adjacent. It also cut the holding panel from 706px to 482px, which is most of what made the panel jump in the first place.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 11.10 | Every asset list goes **two to a row** — the totals panel and all three editors.                                   | Nine holdings were nine lines and a totals panel taller than the diagram beside it. Paired, the same nine read in five rows and the panel fell from 466px to 322px. The same applies to an account's "Assets held", a job's pay assets and a frame's totals: they are one list of one thing, and IBKR's eight were eight lines of a panel that now has a fixed height to fill.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 11.12 | The pairing is decided by a **container query on the panel**, not a media query.                                   | The two widths are inversely related: the panel is at its widest when the page has _stacked_, which is the narrow window — so a media query decides it exactly backwards. Swept with an eight-asset account selected: the row survives down to a 12.9rem container, but by then the amount field is 22px, so the threshold is set by legibility rather than by where the layout breaks. 16rem keeps the field near 56px. The two widths it actually meets are 18.9rem in the two-column layout and 35rem stacked — the first attempt put it at 22rem and silently unpaired the two-column case, which is the layout the report came from.                                                                                                                                                |
| 11.13 | The node's name sits **beside its kind badge**, with no label of its own.                                          | The badge already says "Account"; a "Name" label above the box next to it named the same thing twice and cost a row of a panel that has a fixed height to fill. The accessible name moves onto the input, which is what a screen reader reads either way.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| 11.14 | In and out are shown **side by side**.                                                                             | They are one comparison, not two facts, and a comparison reads across rather than down. 194px each in the two-column layout.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 11.15 | The panel's height is set so **nothing scrolls**, and spacing was tightened first.                                 | Measured across all 24 nodes with the height released: 482px for every holding, job and small account, 617px for the eight-asset one. Tightening the block gap and dropping a section-title margin that duplicated it brought the tallest to 567px. The three-row notes textarea adds about 44px to every node and flow panel, so the tallest is now about **611px of the 620px available**: computed rather than re-measured, so none should scroll, but the room for another pair of assets is gone. `overflow-y: auto` stays as the valve — a far larger account scrolls rather than losing rows off the bottom.                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 11.16 | The page scrolls everywhere **except over the canvas**, which zooms.                                               | The side panels had `overscroll-behavior: contain`, added so a scrolled panel would not drag the page with it. But an `overflow: auto` box is a scroll container even when its content fits, and `contain` stops the wheel there rather than passing it on — so the page would not scroll while the pointer was anywhere over the right-hand column, which after 11.15 is a column that never scrolls itself. Removed. The canvas remains the one exception, and it earns it by handling the event: measured, the page stayed at 616px while the zoom went 83% → 75%.                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 11.17 | The editing actions ride **beside the title**; the diagram selector and the backup controls sit **on the canvas**. | 11.7 found the real ceiling on the diagram: not the canvas height but the 529px of page furniture above it — title, subtitle, a tab row, a backup row and a toolbar panel, each on a line of its own, in a 791px viewport. The actions are page-level and belong with the page's name; which diagram and what to export are about the canvas underneath, so they sit on it. Everything page-level ended up on the title's line — the diagram tabs at its left, the editing actions and the backup controls at its right — and the workspace row starts immediately under it, so the canvas and the panel beside it now share a top edge as well as a bottom one (123px and 1123px, both exact). Furniture fell from **529px to 123px** and the canvas grew from 520px to 1000px.         |
| 11.18 | Screen text that only restates what the screen shows was **removed**.                                              | The subtitle told a reader already on the page what the page is. The idle canvas hint — "Drag nodes to arrange them, the canvas to move around, and scroll to zoom" — told a user with a pointer on the canvas what moving it would teach, and at 676px it was wider than the 661px the bar had left, so it took a whole line to do it. The three mode hints stay — they say what the app is waiting for, which nothing else does — but they moved onto the canvas beside the save readout: appearing above it would resize the row mid-gesture and take the alignment with it. Verified: entering connect mode leaves the canvas at 123–1123px, unmoved.                                                                                                                                |
| 11.19 | The header is **one line at every width**, and its controls are sized to it.                                       | Wrapping put the actions under the title and moved the canvas down a whole row — the same layout shift 11.17 had just removed, arriving from the other direction. `nowrap` means the row has to fit instead: the nine controls inherited the 20px page font against the tabs' 15.2px beside them, which is what made it too wide, so they drop to 0.68rem with tighter padding — 114px to 78px each, the actions group from 1032px to 737px. The tabs scroll rather than push anything off the line, and the header centres rather than top-aligning, since with no subtitle `flex-start` left the controls 10px above the word they line up with. Swept from 2276px down to 700px: 53px tall and one row throughout, with the canvas still sharing both edges with the panel beside it. |
| 11.20 | A balance says what is **committed** as well as what is left.                                                      | 1.000 with 900 already promised read exactly like 1.000 with nothing promised. `selectAllocation` reuses `committed`, so a percentage can never disagree with the totals computed from the same flows. Silent when nothing is committed: a chip that always says 100% is noise. Measured on the real document — LinkedIn's USD reads 65%, "3250,00 free of 4983,33".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 11.21 | Over-allocation is marked **on the node**, in colour rather than in a new row.                                     | It is the one thing about a balance you should not have to open a panel to learn, and the boxes are sized to their content with 1px of slack — there is no room for a row. An account's `remaining` is already net of what its holdings promised, so a negative _is_ the fact; a job's balances are gross, which is why the canvas passes the set explicitly. Node heights are untouched: 24 nodes, 3px worst slack, unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 11.22 | A flow can be **executed**, and a refusal says why in words.                                                       | Ported from the legacy's `canExecuteEdge` rather than reinvented — each of its refusals is a rule about money we would otherwise learn again the hard way. `executeFlow` returns the diagram untouched when refused, so a caller that skipped the check cannot half-apply a transfer. It runs through the existing history with no coalesce key: settling is a discrete act and each one is its own step to undo.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 11.23 | An executed flow **keeps its place and loses its amount**.                                                         | The legacy's answer (`edge.amount = 0`), and the right one for a diagram: the shape of where money goes is worth keeping after a particular transfer has happened. Deleting the flow would make the drawing forget its own structure every time it was used.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 11.24 | Converting between assets is **refused, not guessed**.                                                             | The legacy declined cross-currency transfers rather than inventing a rate, and nothing here has one either. The refusal names both assets, so the reason is the answer rather than a dead end.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 11.25 | Every amount field holds six figures, and the room came from what was not using it.                                | Measured against `999.999,99`: the per-asset amount had 69px of the 96 it needs and the fee fields 71, while the holding amount had 376. The symbol track beside them was a flat 84px for a widest ticker of 31. Cutting it to 2.4rem funded the asset amount entirely; the fee fields took theirs from the unit picker, which only ever shows "%" or "flat". Margins now 9px, 4px and 280px, with the panel still scrollbar-free.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 11.26 | The allocation is one row on the node: **symbol, remaining / total, share**.                                       | Drawn where the balance is rather than left to the panel — how much of a balance is still yours to move is the question the diagram exists to answer. One row at 8px, not two at 11: measured, the worst case a real document can produce (a four-letter ticker with six figures on both sides) comes to 167px of the 180 available, where 9px gave 186 and would have wrapped into a line `allocatedHeight` never reserved. It costs no more height than the chip it replaces.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 11.27 | Amount fields are **text with `inputMode="decimal"`**, not `type="number"`.                                        | A native number field renders its value with the browser UI's separator and will not be overridden — so the same balance read `3250,00` on a node and `3250.00` in the field that edited it, and it refuses `setSelectionRange`, so there was no fixing it from outside either. A typed point becomes a comma: on a numeric keypad the point is the only separator there is, and it should mean what the screen means by it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 11.28 | `parseAmount` treats a second separator as a **typo, not a thousands mark**.                                       | Reading `1.234,56` as 1234.56 would mean reading a mistyped `1.5` as 15. Nothing writes grouping into an input, so the safe reading of an ambiguous string is "unfinished" — and `amountToInput` writes no grouping either, so everything it produces round-trips unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 11.29 | Refusal messages go through the app's own formatter.                                                               | Found by reading one on screen: "this moves 1234.56" was the single number in the app still written with a point, in the message that exists to be read carefully. Numbers interpolated raw into a string are outside every convention until someone looks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 11.30 | The kind label moved to the **corner, out of the flow**.                                                           | It was a full 16px row plus its gap on every node, spent telling you something the colour and the shape already say. Absolute in the node's top padding instead, at 8px — which is why the padding is asymmetric, 13 above and 8 below, and why `geometry.ts` and the stylesheet have to agree on those two numbers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 11.31 | A holding is its **figure**, with its asset in the corner.                                                         | Its name _was_ its asset, so the node said the same word twice and paid a row for the repeat. What is left in the body is the amount. It went to 22px first and that was too far — a five-figure amount filled almost the whole box, which reads as shouting rather than as emphasis. `FONT.amount = 12` still leads the **93px** holding box without owning it, and takes the holding from 84px to **42**, on more than half the nodes in the diagram.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 11.32 | The name is smaller than the numbers it introduces.                                                                | 15px down to 12, against an amount that went 16 up to 22. A name is a label; the figure is the point. Balance chips went 11 to 12 and no further — two share an 86px column, and 13 pushed the widest amount past it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 11.33 | `MIN_NODE_HEIGHT` came down to 42, twice.                                                                          | Once with the corner label and again with the smaller holding figure. At 74 it padded three quarters of the diagram back to a height none of them needed; at 48 it was still adding 7px of air to every bare holding, measured — the flat sizing this section set out to remove, arriving through the back door. At 42 the worst slack across 28 nodes is 2px.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 11.34 | The allocation row is 10px, and the ceiling is the node's width.                                                   | `FONT.allocation = 10`. `geometry.ts` carries the measured sweep rather than borrowing the chip font: 8px reaches 164px, 9px reaches 183px and overflows, and 10px reaches 202px at the extreme. Ten is the last safe size; the one case that does not fit — a four-letter ticker with six figures on _both_ sides — is clipped by `.allocations` rather than allowed to paint over a neighbour, and the reserved height does not move either way.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 11.35 | A job's width follows its **content**, the way an account's does.                                                  | A job was a flat 240px whatever it held. `sizeOf` now passes a measured `minimumWidth` through `contentWidth`, clamped to `NODE_WIDTH`, so no caller can reserve more than the node it draws. On the real document LinkedIn is 217px with one allocation row; Polisense and RAVN are 115px for “No assets yet”; and single-chip accounts are 124px.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 11.36 | The chip block reserves **both columns** it draws.                                                                 | `.balances` is `repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr))` with a 3px by 7px gap, but it was measured as one chip: a two-asset account reserved 124px, drew two roughly 47px columns and ellipsised both amounts. Width is now `widest × columns + 7 × (columns − 1)`; a lone chip spans both columns through `:only-child`, so reserved width is never less than drawn width. Two chips measure 233px.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 11.37 | An account's width is **not** its holdings' span.                                                                  | This explicitly reverses b50c5b8 and a46a8e2: a holding's position is layout, not account content. Scotiabank's two holdings sit 18px apart and BCP's 194px apart while both accounts say the same two chips; the span made them 233px and 240px. Those seven pixels carried no information and changed whenever a holding moved. An account is now a function of its own rows, which also removes per-holding `sizeOf` recursion from every canvas render and prevents dragging a holding from resizing its account.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 11.38 | Notes are a **box**, not a line.                                                                                   | A one-line input made a running log scroll sideways: Fondos BCP reads “10/07 - 2000 10/08 - 2011.90” and RAVN “07/08 - USD +785.58”. Nodes and flows now share a `textarea` with `rows={3}`, a two-line floor and `resize: vertical`; the panel may take height, which it already scrolls, but never width, because that width belongs to the canvas beside it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 11.11 | The two paragraphs explaining fee order were **removed**.                                                          | "Out counts what left…" and "A transfer takes the source's out fee first…" described mechanics the panel already shows: the After-fees chain names each step and its deduction as it happens. A rule stated in prose beside the same rule shown in numbers is one of them too many, and it was the taller half of the fee section.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

---

### 12. Airfare

| ID                              | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 12.1                            | Airfare is a **fifth tab**, not panels on an existing page.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Decision 2.6 refused Pulse a route because Pulse is markets data on a markets page. Airfare shares nothing with any existing tab: not a domain object, not a provider, not a store. Folding it into Investing would be putting flights on a page about equities because both have prices, which is the reasoning 8.5 already rejected for holdings and positions.
