| 12.265                          | Every field sits **beside its label**, in one label column, and the airports stop sharing a row.                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Asked for by the owner, and the second half is what it costs. A label beside a field takes the label's width, and at the panel's 20rem floor — 400px, so 358px of content at this app's 125% root where 1rem is 20px and not 16 — two labels plus two inputs at their 8rem minimum plus chrome and gaps runs well over 500px, and the overflow would land on the one page in this app that must never scroll sideways. Three rows of one field each is a line _shorter_ than the stacked arrangement it replaces and lines the three inputs up on one left edge. The column is `auto`, so it is as wide as "Destination" says and no wider, and `AirportField` joins it through `display: contents` rather than a grid of its own — three nested grids would each size to their own label and start the three inputs at three different x positions. Measured at the floor in a browser: the widest row is 358px and nothing scrolls.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 12.266                          | The server-side focus goes **with it**: off the collect payload, and out of the scheduler's ordering.                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Argued rather than left. `focusDate` on `RouteBody` was the one reading preference this API ever accepted, and the only thing it did was feed `due_now`'s `focused` set (12.134). With no focus in the client model nothing can set it, so keeping the field would leave a parameter no caller sets in front of a sort key that is the same for every candidate — an unreachable branch, and the scheduler is exactly where one is worst, because the ordering it decides is invisible until a budget bites. So `RouteBody.focusDate`, `FareWatch.focus`, `Due.focused`, `due_now(focused=…)` and `collect_due`'s containment check all go together, and the sort returns to `(not ready, days_out)` — 12.111, which is the rule the focus was jumping ahead of and which was never removed. Two things deliberately not done: the 422 for a focus outside its month is not kept (there is no month-and-day pair left to be inconsistent), and a stale client still sending the key is **ignored** rather than refused, which is Pydantic's default and is right here — the value would only have changed the order of a pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 12.268                          | Origin and Destination go back on **one row**, and the width comes off the **input** rather than the labels.                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Asked for by the owner after seeing 12.265 split them, and the measurement that made splitting look necessary is what says where to find the width. A label beside a field costs the label, and two labels plus two inputs at the old `min-width: 8rem` ran over 500px against the 358 this panel has at its 20rem floor — but 8rem is **160px held for a three-letter IATA code**. `LIM` is 31.65px at 0.9rem, so with the horizontal padding down from `--space-3` to `--space-2` the field spends 53.65px of the 62.75 the row can give it, and the two labels never had to move: `Departing` 84.38 and `Destination` 103.13 are the fixed costs, three 15px gaps are the rest, and 358 − 84.38 − 103.13 − 45 = 125.49 for the pair. The form is four tracks and the airports row is a `subgrid` of them, so "Origin" and "Departing" share the first column and the origin code and the month dropdown start at the same x — a nested grid would measure its own labels and land somewhere else. **The suggestion lists move with it, and that is not cosmetic.** A label beside a field pushes the input 99px in from the panel's edge, and a 17rem list opening rightwards from there was measured 90px past the viewport: a horizontal scrollbar on the page that must never have one, introduced by 12.265 and caught here. `layout="inline"` now also means `.control` stops being the positioning context and the caller's row is it, so both lists reach the _form's_ edges and are capped at its width — 358px at the floor, flush both sides, for any city name in the table. `min-width` beats `max-width` in CSS, so the 17rem floor carries the same ceiling or it would walk straight through it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 12.269                          | A watchlist longer than the map **scrolls inside the row they share**, rather than growing it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | 276530a's finding for Investing, applied to the same shape here — and it is worth restating because the wrong half looks like the fix. `flex: 1 1 0` on the scroller does not stop the rows pushing the panel taller: the intrinsic height of a column flex container comes from its items' max-content _contributions_, and a growable item contributes all of its content however small its basis, while a scroll container's automatic _minimum_ size is zero but its max-content size is still everything inside it. `contain: size` is what says "size this box as if it were empty". Measured with a synthetic twenty-route watchlist at the 20rem floor: 901px of rows in a 349px box, and the two panels 593px each rather than the map ending 300px above the watchlist. The fields stay outside the scroller, for the reason the Investing rail gives — a list long enough to scroll is exactly when you least want the way to add to it scrolled away. Stacked, containment comes **off**: a box sized as if it were empty inside a panel with no height to hand down is a box of no height, and the rows would vanish instead of scrolling, so they size themselves again and stop at a 32rem ceiling. The three values are custom properties set in `AirfarePage.module.css` and read in `RouteList.module.css`, because the width at which the page stacks is that file's decision and a second copy of `1080px` in the other is precisely the number nobody remembers to move twice.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 12.270                          | The provider's far edge is a **date it will price up to**, not a distance from today, so the horizon constant is right when measured and wrong the next morning.                                                                                                                                               | Measured 2026-08-20: a window ending +330 days out was refused where the same window ending +329 answered in full, and the day before that +330 had answered. One day's sample cannot tell a shrinking limit from a fixed date, so `MAX_DEPARTURE_HORIZON_DAYS` is left alone and the collector stops depending on it being exact.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 12.271                          | A refused far end is **walked back and asked again**, in doubling steps, bounded.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Reporting the refusal and giving up loses the whole curve for the sake of one day at its end — every day, forever, since the edge moves a day closer each morning. Doubling finds an edge a month adrift in five extra requests and costs one in the ordinary case; the bound means a provider that has stopped answering is reported rather than probed at. Only the answered window is stored, and the curve records its own `from`/`to`, so a horizon that fell short says so on disk.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| 12.272                          | The range refusal carries **its own error code**, `range-refused`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | It is the one refusal a caller can answer, by asking for less. Sharing `upstream-error` with parse failures and consent pages meant the retry would have had to match on the message text — a behaviour hinging on a sentence.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| frame-is-dragged                | The departure chart's frame is **panned by dragging it**, and the viewport it is panned to is one of the things a route remembers.                                                                                                                                                                             | The chart draws a whole booking horizon into one frame and a reader who wants a fortnight of it had, until now, to change the period to get there — which changes what a period _is_ for the flight table below as well, so a reader asking a question about the chart moved the table underneath it. A drag asks nothing of anything else. `lib/viewport.ts` owns the arithmetic and clamps the frame to the archive at both ends, so the horizon cannot be dragged into empty space, and a press stays a click until the pointer has travelled `DRAG_SLOP`: reading the crosshair and panning out of one gesture would have the hairline chase the dots it was sliding past and the readout flicker through twenty itineraries on the way. The drag measures every move against the viewport's start at the moment the press landed rather than against the previous move — summing deltas accumulates each one's rounding and the frame then fails to come back to where it started when the hand does, a drift that reads as the chart sliding on its own.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| reading-belongs-to-route        | How a route is read — its period, the departure day chart B is anchored on, and that viewport — is held **per route**, in `useRouteView`, rather than as state inside the panel that draws it.                                                                                                                 | The anchor was state in `AnalysisPanel`, paired with the route it belonged to and cleared on every change of route. That was the right answer while there was nothing to restore it _to_: an anchor is a day of _one_ watch, and carrying it to the next route would name a departure that route does not have. But a reader comparing two watches opens the second and comes back, and finding the first one reset to its own first period is the panel forgetting something the reader did not. A route that remembers how it was last read does not need its anchor cleared, it needs it looked up — so the clearing and the route-tracking state both go, and the panel takes the answer as a prop. It is still a _day_ rather than an index into the periods, for 12.143's reason: the period switch rebuilds the periods under it, and an index kept across a week → day flip points at the seventh day of the month instead of at the day being read.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| period-switch-follows-its-chart | The period switch **unfolds from chart B's button** and folds away with it, and the space it takes is held open either way.                                                                                                                                                                                    | Chart A is drawn by day and by nothing else — 12.242 — so beside it the switch was a live control that moved nothing next to it, and the reader's reading of that was the correct one: it looks broken. Removing it from the layout on chart A is the other wrong answer, and worse: the head is `space-between` and the switch sits at its right edge, so a group that left the layout would slide the two chart buttons under the reader's hand at the exact moment they pressed one — the reflow 12.240 refused, arriving by the other door. Held open and hidden, the buttons cannot move a pixel, which is the mechanism the chart's own name already uses to change without moving (12.246). `visibility` rather than opacity alone, because a control the reader cannot see must also be one Tab cannot reach and a screen reader does not read. It is still never a lie about what it governs: the flight table below is grouped by the same period and says so on its own heading. | S.13 | 12.190's "the horizon is a fourth view in the same switch" replaced by 12.203, which is the answer 12.197 was written to leave open — it recorded that flights and departure dates were one axis at two scales and deliberately did not act, pending this decision. 12.191 through 12.196 and 12.198 all stand: the axis is still which departure date, the periods are still 331/48/12, the two absences are still two marks, the dots still appear by spacing arithmetic, and the curve is still fetched beside the archive on the page. Only its place in the switch moved.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| S.14 | 12.172's "lead time is a third view", 12.173's 1/7/30-day meaning and 12.174's two-series rule are all withdrawn by 12.241, along with 12.202 and 12.204. S.12 had already replaced the _view_ with a labelling control; what goes now is the reading itself. 12.174's substance survives everywhere it was really about — two series stay two series on chart A, and a period nobody observed is still drawn as nothing rather than as a line across it — but it survives as `PriceBandChart`'s own rule and not as a lead-time one. 12.173's units and 12.204's explanation have nothing left to describe.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| S.15 | 12.203's "one axis with a zoom" replaced by 12.243. Its reason stands and is why the two ends were merged at all: the watched month and the booking horizon are the same question — which departure date — at two scales, and 12.140's separate views were wrong about that. What it could not see is that a control was still the wrong way to move between them. Which archive holds a departure date is a property of the date, so the reader was being asked to answer something the page already knew; and no position of the zoom could draw the period that has one foot on each side of the boundary. 12.192's "no previous-next arrows on the horizon" goes with it — there is one frame at a time now and arrows are how a reader reaches the next.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| S.16 | 12.235's clip of the frame to the watched month replaced by 12.243. It was right for exactly as long as the boards were the only thing that could be drawn: a week overhanging into April asserted four departure dates nothing had ever been collected about. The horizon prices those dates now, so cropping them would be the chart hiding data it holds. The watched range still decides which dates the _boards_ may speak for — `departureFrame.frameDays` — which is the same fact put to the use it was always for; what is superseded is only that it also cropped the frame.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| S.17 | 12.234's "under a pointer both the hairline and the plate stay" replaced by 12.245. The rest of 12.234 stands and is now the whole rule rather than the keyboard's half: a period with no figure gets no horizontal hairline and no price plate. The exception existed because under a pointer the hairline was a ruler the reader was holding at a height of their own choosing — and it is not one any more, so there is nothing left for it to report.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| S.18 | 12.190 through 12.198 lose the component they described: `CalendarCurveChart` is deleted by 12.243, and `lib/calendarCurve.ts` keeps only `eachDate` and `collectedAtLabel`. 12.191's "the axis is which departure date" and 12.195's two absences both survive in full on the chart that replaced it. What does not is 12.192's 331/48/12 folding — a curve date is drawn as itself now, so there is no week-of-curve-dates to summarise — and with it 12.193's separate reading type, 12.194's one-dimensional snap and 12.196's dot-spacing arithmetic, all of which existed to draw a folded curve. 12.198 stands untouched: the curve is still fetched beside the archive on the page and still not gated on the open view.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

A point is what it used to change; a period is what it changes now.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| S.20 | 12.244's "a label under each stretch naming which archive answered" replaced by 12.251: a label under each _archive_, on the widest stretch it holds. The rest of 12.244 stands untouched — the span rather than the point, the axis changing resolution where the source does, the seam on the boundary midnight, Day bounded to the watched month and Month never mixing. What was wrong was only the count: a run and an archive are the same thing on a two-run frame, and every frame anyone had drawn was a two-run frame. | S.23 | 12.134 and 12.135 replaced by 12.266. 12.134's ordering — the focused departure kept first when a budget truncates a pass — has nothing left that could enter its set, so it is removed rather than left as a constant sort key, and `due_now` returns to 12.111's nearest-first. 12.135's finding outlives the decision it was written for and is not superseded with it: a departure 150 days out moves on 22% of days by a median 1.7%, ordering is free and rate is not, and `poll_minutes` never knew the focus existed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 2026-08-19 |
| S.24 | 12.180, 12.181 and 12.182 replaced by 12.262 and 12.260. 12.180's exact departure date existed to produce a focus; the form asks for a month and a year now. 12.181's "the model keeps both" has one of the two left to keep. 12.182 gave the detail panel sole ownership of clearing a focus, and the control it named — "Read the whole month" — was the way out of a state nothing can now be in; a panel that only ever reads the whole month has nothing to offer a reader a way back from.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | 2026-08-19 |
| S.25 | 12.185 replaced by 12.262, and the hazard it names is what the replacement is measured against. It made a month and a day unable to disagree rather than guarded against disagreeing, because the disagreement was _invisible_: `type="date"` renders `09/03/2027` identically whichever month sits above it. What made that possible was redundancy — a day states its own month, so two editable values named one fact. A month and a year hold none: neither states the other, together they name exactly one `YYYY-MM`, and both are spelled out on screen. What is left is a combination outside the horizon, which is a different thing and is refused in words — 12.264.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | 2026-08-19 |
| S.26 | 12.183 is **not** superseded, and is recorded here only because it was written as a limit on 12.180. "The watchlist row still shows the month, not the date the reader picked" is now simply true of everything on the page: the row was the one place that already refused the focus, on the ground that it is the collection list, and it needed no change at all.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | 2026-08-19 |
| S.27 | 12.265's "three rows, one field each" replaced by 12.268: the airports share a row again. The rest of 12.265 stands and is what 12.268 is built on — every field still sits beside its own label in one label column, and the arithmetic it recorded is what located the width. What is superseded is only its conclusion that two labels and two fields cannot fit, which was true of the 8rem input it measured and not of the field a three-letter code actually needs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 2026-08-20 |
| S.28 | 12.241 already withdrew the run-up reading, for a reason the owner gave directly, and deleted `leadTime.ts` with it — so this branch's own row saying the same thing is withdrawn rather than merged. It said the reading went because nothing could name a departure for it to run up to and that the library was **kept**; the first is a second account of one removal and the second is now simply false. What it recorded that 12.241 does not is folded into this row: 12.204's gate is not merely unset but unreachable, because 12.260 leaves no watch that can name one departure. The general run-splitting in `lib/departureFrame.ts` is deliberately **not** narrowed to match — it takes a date range rather than a route, a month-wide watch simply never builds the three-run frame, and a rule cut down to the inputs one caller happens to send is a rule that breaks the next time another does. The frames that found the rail defect stay in its suite and in `DepartureChart`'s, described as ranges rather than as focused watches. | 2026-08-20 |
